I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to check if every element in an array is an integer or a float with no decimals
2. The function should return `True` if:
   - The array is empty (which is considered valid)
   - Every element is an integer or a float with no decimal part
3. It should return `False` for any other input

## Step-by-step Analysis

1. **Empty array**: Return `True` (as specified in the problem)
2. **Non-empty array**: Check each element to see if it's either:
   - An integer (type `int`)
   - A float that has no decimal part (like 3.0, 5.0, etc.)

3. **How to check if a float has no decimal part**:
   - Convert float to integer and compare with original
   - If `float_value == int(float_value)`, then it has no decimal part
   - Alternatively, check if `float_value.is_integer()` (which is available in Python 3)

4. **Edge cases**:
   - Negative numbers (like -3.0, -2.5)
   - NaN values
   - Strings or other types

## Implementation Strategy

1. Handle empty array immediately
2. For non-empty array, iterate through each element
3. For each element, check if it's a number (int or float)
4. If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part
5. If any element fails the check, return `False`
6. If all elements pass, return `True`

## Code Implementation

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # Empty array returns True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is a number (int or float)
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
            
        # If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float):
            if not element.is_integer():
                return False
    
    return True
```

## Verification

- `[]` → `True` ✓ (empty array)
- `[1, 2, 3]` → `True` ✓ (all integers)
- `[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]` → `True` ✓ (all floats with no decimals)
- `[1.1, 2.0, 3]` → `False` ✓ (1.1 has decimal)
- `[1, 2.5, 3]` → `False` ✓ (2.5 has decimal)
- `[1, "hello"]` → `False` ✓ (string type)
- `[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]` → `True` ✓ (all floats with no decimals)

The solution handles all the requirements correctly.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to check if every element in an array is an integer or a float with no decimals
2. The function should return `True` if:
   - The array is empty, OR
   - Every element is an integer or a float with no decimal part
3. It should return `False` for all other cases

## Key Requirements

- Check if elements are integers or floats with no decimals (like 3.0, 5.0, but not 3.1, 4.5)
- Empty array should return True
- Any element that is not a number or has decimals should return False

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Handle empty array**: If the array is empty, return True
2. **Check each element**: For each element, determine if it's a valid integer-like number (integer or float with no decimal part)
3. **Validation logic**: 
   - Check if element is a number (int or float)
   - If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part (e.g., 3.0, 4.0, but not 3.1)
4. **Return result**: If all elements pass the check, return True; otherwise, return False

## Implementation Details

For a float to have no decimal part, we can check if `float.is_integer()` is True, or check if the value equals its integer part.

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # Empty array returns True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is a number (int or float)
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # For float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float) and not element.is_integer():
            return False
    
    return True
```

The `float.is_integer()` method returns True if the float value is a whole number (like 3.0, 5.0, etc.), and False otherwise.

Let me verify with examples:
- `[1, 2, 3]` → True (all integers)
- `[1.0, 2.0, 3.0]` → True (all floats with no decimals)
- `[1.1, 2.0, 3]` → False (1.1 has decimal)
- `[]` → True (empty array)
- `[1, 2.5, 3]` → False (2.5 has decimal)
- `[1, "2", 3]` → False (string is not a number)

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # Empty array returns True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is a number (int or float)
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # For float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float) and not element.is_integer():
            return False
    
    return True
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to check if every element in an array is an integer or a float with no decimals
2. The function should return `True` if:
   - The array is empty, OR
   - Every element is an integer or a float with no decimal part
3. It should return `False` for any other input

## Step-by-step Analysis

1. **Empty array**: Should return `True` (as specified)
2. **Elements that are integers or floats with no decimals**: 
   - Integer values (like 5, -3, 0)
   - Float values that represent whole numbers (like 3.0, -2.0, 0.0)
3. **Elements that are not integers or whole floats**: 
   - Floats with decimals (like 3.14, 2.5)
   - Strings, lists, dictionaries, etc.

## Key Insight

For a float to have no decimals, we need to check if `int(x) == x`. This works because:
- For integers: `int(5) == 5.0`, so `int(5) == 5.0` is True
- For whole floats: `int(3.0) == 3.0`, so `int(3.0) == 3.0` is True
- For floats with decimals: `int(3.14) == 3.0`, so `int(3.14) == 3.14` is False

## Algorithm

1. Handle empty array: return `True`
2. Iterate through each element in the array
3. For each element, check if it's a number (int or float)
4. If it's not a number, return `False`
5. If it's a number, check if it has no decimal part by comparing `int(element) == element`
6. If all elements pass, return `True`

## Edge Cases to Consider

- Empty array → True
- Array with only integers → True
- Array with only whole floats (like 3.0, -2.0) → True
- Array with mixed types (int, float with decimals) → False
- Array with non-numeric types (strings, lists) → False

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # Empty array returns True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is a number (int or float)
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # Check if it's a whole number (no decimal part)
        if not (int(element) == element):
            return False
    
    return True
```

            return False

   - The array is empty (returns True)
   - Every element is an integer or a float with no decimal places

- For a float to have no decimals, it should be equivalent to an integer (e.g., 3.0, 5.0)
- We need to check each element individually
- Empty array should return True

## Step-by-step Approach

1. Handle empty array case immediately - return True
2. For non-empty array, iterate through each element
3. For each element, check if it's an integer OR if it's a float that has no decimal part
4. A float has no decimal part if `element.is_integer()` is True (for floats) or if it's equivalent to an integer

## Implementation Details

- For integers: `isinstance(x, int)` returns True
- For floats: we need to check if `x.is_integer()` is True, which returns True only if the float has no fractional part
- We can use `isinstance(x, float)` and then check `x.is_integer()`

## Edge Cases

- Empty array: True
- Array with integers: True
- Array with floats that have no decimals (like 3.0, 5.0): True
- Array with floats that have decimals (like 3.1, 5.5): False
- Array with strings, None, etc.: False

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # Empty array returns True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    # Check each element
    for element in arr:
        # If element is not a number (int or float), return False
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # For float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float) and not element.is_integer():
            return False
    
    return True
```

2. For each element, check if it's either an int or a float
3. If it's a float and has fractional part (not integer), return False
4. If all elements pass, return True

This handles all the requirements specified in the problem.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to check if every element in an array is an integer or a float with no decimals
2. The function should return `True` if:
   - The array is empty (returns True)
   - Every element is an integer or a float with no decimal places
3. It should return `False` for any other input

## Step-by-Step Analysis

Let me break down what "float with no decimals" means:
- Numbers like 5.0, 3.0, 10.0 are considered integers because they have no decimal part
- Numbers like 5.1, 3.14, 2.01 are not integers because they have decimal parts
- We need to check if each element is either:
  - An integer type (int)
  - A float that is equivalent to an integer (float value with decimal part of 0)

## Key Insights

1. For a float to be considered an integer, its fractional part should be 0
2. We can check if a float `x` has no decimal part by comparing `x == int(x)`
3. We need to handle empty arrays - they should return True
4. We need to handle non-numeric types (strings, lists, etc.) - they should return False

## Algorithm

1. If the array is empty, return True
2. For each element in the array:
   - If it's not a number (not int or float), return False
   - If it's a float, check if it's equal to its integer conversion
   - If it's an int, it's automatically valid
3. If all elements pass the test, return True

## Edge Cases to Consider

- Empty array: should return True
- Single element: must be integer or float with no decimals
- Array with mixed types: should return False
- Array with floating point numbers that have decimal parts: should return False
- Array with floating point numbers that have no decimal parts (like 5.0): should return True

## Implementation

```python
def is_int_array(arr):
    # Empty array returns True
    if not arr:
        return True
    
    for element in arr:
        # Check if element is a number
        if not isinstance(element, (int, float)):
            return False
        
        # If it's a float, check if it has no decimal part
        if isinstance(element, float):
            # A float has no decimal part if it's equal to its integer value
            if element != int(element):
                return False
    
    return True
```

The solution works as follows:
- First check if array is empty - return True
- For each element, verify it's either int or float
- For float elements, check if they are equivalent to their integer value (e.g., 5.0 == 5)
- If any element fails the test, return False
- If all elements pass, return True

This handles all the requirements correctly.
